Epochs are the record's calendar fields, as seconds from 1980-01-06 { "00" }:{ "00" }:{ "00" } in the file's own time system, with no conversion.

# <b>{ "176" } of { "388" }</b> capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } honestly labelled Modelled, { "4" } partner-owned. Each capability carries one label in a machine-checked ledger: VALIDATED (an independent external oracle agrees: real data, an independent implementation or published reference vectors), MODELLED (internally consistent, and said out loud) or PARTNER (a hardware partner owns it). Among the checks: all { "666" } SGP4 (Simplified General Perturbations { "4" }) vectors of AIAA (American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics) 2006-6753 to { "4.12" } mm, and the Cowell force model to { "0.08" } m against Orekit { "12.2" }.
seg-2d2c47db0c1f = <b>{ "176" } van { "388" }</b> mogelijkheden getoetst aan onafhankelijke externe referenties; { "208" } eerlijk gelabeld als modelmatig, { "4" } in eigendom van een partner. Elke mogelijkheid draagt één label in een machinaal gecontroleerd register: VALIDATED (een onafhankelijke externe referentie komt overeen: echte data, een onafhankelijke implementatie of gepubliceerde referentievectoren), MODELLED (intern consistent, en dat wordt openlijk gezegd) of PARTNER (een hardwarepartner is eigenaar). Onder de controles: alle { "666" } SGP4-vectoren (Simplified General Perturbations { "4" }) van AIAA (American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics) 2006-6753 tot { "4.12" } mm, en het Cowell-krachtmodel tot { "0.08" } m ten opzichte van Orekit { "12.2" }.

# Kshana Studio, Simple view, opening screen, titled What would you like to find out?, with the engine { "v0.38.0" } live and, in the top bar, three links: Training debrief, Interference map and Receiver trust. Four questions: how many satellites can I see anywhere on Earth, can I trust my position fix, how long can a clock keep time without satellites, and what does a jammer do to my receiver. Below the first, its recorded result: at least { "22" } satellites in view at every place and { "31" } on average with GPS, Galileo, BeiDou and GLONASS together, a fix possible at { "100" } % of places and times, on a world map of satellites in view, with an Open this result button. # How a run flows. A scenario TOML file (a kind, a seed and its parameters) goes into the engine, kshana { $version }, through the api::run_toml dispatch over { "87" } scenario kinds, deterministic from scenario, seed and engine version. The engine writes result.json, chart.svg, report.html and report.json, a table.csv for the kinds that define one, and on request SP3, CCSDS OMM and OEM, CZML, KML, GeoJSON, STK and SigMF exports; a suite writes study.json and study.html. Those files feed Kshana Studio in the browser, an AI assistant through the kshana-mcp server, and continuous integration.
seg-76e3ec458441 = Hoe een run verloopt. Een scenario-TOML-bestand (een soort, een seed en zijn parameters) gaat de engine in, kshana { $version }, via de api::run_toml-dispatch over { "87" } scenariosoorten, deterministisch vanuit scenario, seed en engineversie. De engine schrijft result.json, chart.svg, report.html en report.json, een table.csv voor de soorten die er een definiëren, en op verzoek exports in SP3, CCSDS OMM en OEM, CZML, KML, GeoJSON, STK en SigMF; een suite schrijft study.json en study.html. Die bestanden voeden Kshana Studio in de browser, een AI-assistent via de kshana-mcp-server en continue integratie.

# <b>{ "176" } of { "388" }</b> capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } honestly labelled Modelled. Each row of the verification matrix names a capability, the oracle it is checked against, the test that runs the check, and the label that follows. Continuous integration (CI) makes it impossible to call a capability VALIDATED without an independent external oracle.
seg-707e2cf795f6 = <b>{ "176" } van de { "388" }</b> mogelijkheden getoetst aan onafhankelijke externe referenties; { "208" } eerlijk gelabeld als berekend uit een model. Elke rij van de verificatiematrix noemt een mogelijkheid, de onafhankelijke referentie waaraan ze wordt getoetst, de test die de controle uitvoert en het label dat daaruit volgt. Continue integratie (CI) maakt het onmogelijk een mogelijkheid VALIDATED te noemen zonder een onafhankelijke externe referentie.

# How a capability earns its label. Each row of the verification matrix names a capability, the oracle it is checked against, the test that runs the check in continuous integration, and the label that follows. A row may be VALIDATED only with an independent external oracle; otherwise it is MODELLED, or PARTNER when a hardware partner owns it. Example: SGP4/SDP4, Cowell 6-DOF + perturbations, batch/sequential OD, oracle AIAA 2006-6753 SGP4 verification vectors, test tests/sgp4_verification.rs, label VALIDATED. Live counts: { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER of { "388" } rows. In all, { "176" } capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } more are honestly labelled MODELLED and { "4" } are PARTNER-owned.
seg-2f81cf391eae = Hoe een mogelijkheid haar label verdient. Elke rij van de verificatiematrix noemt een mogelijkheid, de onafhankelijke referentie waaraan ze wordt getoetst, de test die de controle in continue integratie uitvoert en het label dat daaruit volgt. Een rij mag alleen VALIDATED zijn met een onafhankelijke externe referentie; anders is ze MODELLED, of PARTNER wanneer een hardwarepartner haar bezit. Voorbeeld: SGP4/SDP4, Cowell 6-DOF + verstoringen, batch-/sequentiële baanbepaling, referentie AIAA 2006-6753 SGP4-verificatievectoren, test tests/sgp4_verification.rs, label VALIDATED. Actuele aantallen: { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER van { "388" } rijen. In totaal zijn { "176" } mogelijkheden getoetst aan onafhankelijke externe referenties; { "208" } andere zijn eerlijk gelabeld als MODELLED en { "4" } zijn eigendom van een PARTNER.

# Kshana's architecture: one open engine at the centre, kshana { $version } under the AGPL-3.0, with api::run_toml, a typed dispatch over every kind, and the verification ledger of { "388" } capabilities ({ "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER). Around it, every surface runs the same engine: Command line (cargo install kshana); Rust library (cargo add kshana); Python (pip install kshana); WebAssembly + Kshana Studio (npm install kshana); MCP server (cargo install kshana-mcp); Docker image (ghcr.io/ashfordeou/kshana-mcp); JetBrains plugin (Marketplace: "Kshana"). Below it, Kshana Pro, a proprietary overlay that depends on the open engine as a library and never forks it, and adds no physical model.
seg-9d69cf43cd40 = De architectuur van Kshana: één open engine in het midden, kshana { $version } onder de AGPL-3.0, met api::run_toml, een getypeerde dispatch over elke soort, en het verificatieregister van { "388" } mogelijkheden ({ "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER). Eromheen draait elk oppervlak dezelfde engine: opdrachtregel (cargo install kshana); Rust-bibliotheek (cargo add kshana); Python (pip install kshana); WebAssembly + Kshana Studio (npm install kshana); MCP-server (cargo install kshana-mcp); Docker-image (ghcr.io/ashfordeou/kshana-mcp); JetBrains-plugin (Marketplace: „Kshana”). Eronder staat Kshana Pro, een propriëtaire overlay die als bibliotheek van de open engine afhangt, hem nooit forkt en geen fysisch model toevoegt.

# Kshana's architecture in layers. Five front doors (command line, Python, JavaScript and WebAssembly, the MCP server and the JetBrains plugin) reach one api::run_toml, a typed dispatch over { "87" } kinds. Beneath it sit eight domain layers: time and frames, clocks and timing, inertial and fusion, GNSS and integrity, astrodynamics, LEO PNT, Moon, Mars and deep space, and missions and studies, on a shared core, all cross-referenced by the verification module, the machine-checked matrix of { "388" } capabilities that is the single source of truth for every label.
seg-9bc9a971bdb4 = De architectuur van Kshana in lagen. Vijf toegangspoorten (opdrachtregel, Python, JavaScript en WebAssembly, de MCP-server en de JetBrains-plugin) bereiken één api::run_toml, een getypeerde dispatch over { "87" } soorten. Daaronder liggen acht domeinlagen: tijd en referentiestelsels, klokken en tijdsynchronisatie, traagheid en fusie, GNSS en integriteit, astrodynamica, LEO-PNT, maan, Mars en diepe ruimte, en missies en studies, op een gedeelde kern, alle kruiselings verwezen door de verificatiemodule, de machinaal gecontroleerde matrix van { "388" } mogelijkheden die de enige bron van waarheid is voor elk label.

# <b>{ "176" } of { "388" }</b> capabilities validated against independent external oracles; { "208" } honestly labelled Modelled, { "4" } partner-owned. Each capability carries one label in a machine-checked ledger: VALIDATED (an independent external oracle agrees: real data, an independent implementation or published reference vectors), MODELLED (internally consistent, and said out loud) or PARTNER (a hardware partner owns it). Among the checks: all { "666" } SGP4 (Simplified General Perturbations { "4" }) vectors of AIAA (American Institute of Aeronautics and Astronautics) 2006-6753 to { "4.12" } mm, and the Cowell force model to { "0.08" } m against Orekit { "12.2" }.

Elke mogelijkheid draagt één label in een machinaal gecontroleerd register: VALIDATED (een onafhankelijke externe referentie komt overeen: echte data, een onafhankelijke implementatie of gepubliceerde referentievectoren), MODELLED (intern consistent, en dat wordt openlijk gezegd) of PARTNER (een hardwarepartner is eigenaar). # The complete, machine-checked evidence ledger: <b>{ "388" } rows — { "176" } VALIDATED, { "208" } MODELLED, { "4" } PARTNER</b>. # Three independent tools, pre-registered (0123cee1) before any fixture or oracle output existed, tolerances fixed then. ({ "1" }) ARAIM: Stanford MAAST for ARAIM { "2" } (commit ab70e2a3, BSD-3) mhss_raim_baseline_v5.m, run as a separate program under GNU Octave { "8.4" } with a driver-side unique() compatibility shim (disclosed, no algorithm change), at the ADD TOL_PL of { "0.05" } m: the Rust reproduces the ADD reference implementation on { "270" } real cases. Disclosed: the Kshana ADD path was written after the pre-registration, following the ADD as MAAST implements it; an independent hold-out by the reviewer ({ "270" } new user geometries and a second integrity support message, MAAST re-run against the frozen code) passed at the same bar. ({ "2" }) Snapshot RAIM: RTKLIB v2.4.2-p13 (BSD-2) estpos/valsol/raim_fde in a separate C harness for detection and exclusion; the slope PL from RTKLIB matinv/xyz2enu plus SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2, where both sides evaluate the same closed form (Brown; Parkinson and Axelrad), so the PL check covers the implementation, not the formula. ({ "3" }) SBAS: Stanford MAAST (commit 7d32b049) usr_vhpl, unmodified, on MAAST own recorded WAAS broadcasts: L1 levels within { "1e-4" } m after the K rescaling and an equal protected set. The same comparisons produced the findings stated in the capability: the uniform-sigma ARAIM functions are not ADD-conformant, and the L5 set equality fails on { "308" } pairs with no GEO in view (the levels on the { "3267" } pairs both tools protect agree to { "1e-13" } m). The DO-229E/DO-316 K-factors are transcribed constants and the real geometry is an input; neither is counted as an oracle. { "0.30" } revision: snapshot_raim protection levels moved from the radial to the geodetic local level (up to { "0.31" } m HPL and { "0.14" } m VPL at { "16" } deg N)

# Two internal oracles, no external dataset. ({ "1" }) Closed-form round trip: the minimum detectable bias is produced by inverting the non-central chi-square tail on the non-centrality (raim::pbias) and is verified by feeding the resulting non-centrality back through raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf at the monitor's own threshold, which must return P_md. ({ "2" }) Injection vs analysis: a bias is written into the RF estimate of one axis and cross_raim::run_cross_raim is re-run, so the statistic compared against the analytic non-centrality is the one the monitor computed, not a re-derivation. A seeded Monte-Carlo of the statistic itself is carried in the tests as a third check; it is deliberately NOT in the report, because a sampled estimate would be slower and not reproducible bit-for-bit. ExternalDataset is declined: the quantity is the detection power of THIS monitor at THIS scenario's sigma allocation, and the sigma magnitudes the MDB is scaled by are Modelled representative inputs, not measurements. ReferenceImpl was also considered and declined — the Monte-Carlo samples the same statistic the analysis describes, so it catches transcription and coefficient errors but is not an independent implementation of the monitor. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered 0135a9e4), blocked (stays MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduce the report threshold (within { "5.4e-12" }), per-axis MDB, power curve, crossing multiple and ramp time-to-detect (within { "5.3e-10" }) on five configurations; the injected-fault statistics have no independent oracle and the sigma magnitudes are Modelled inputs, so the scoping awaits an owner decision
seg-b6b8dec4dcff = Twee interne onafhankelijke referenties, geen externe dataset. ({ "1" }) Round trip in gesloten vorm: de minimaal detecteerbare bias wordt voortgebracht door de niet-centrale chikwadraatstaart te inverteren op de niet-centraliteit (raim::pbias) en wordt geverifieerd door de resulterende niet-centraliteit terug te voeren door raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf bij de eigen drempel van de monitor, wat P_md moet teruggeven. ({ "2" }) Injectie tegenover analyse: een bias wordt in de RF-schatting van één as geschreven en cross_raim::run_cross_raim wordt opnieuw uitgevoerd, zodat de statistiek die met de analytische niet-centraliteit wordt vergeleken, die is die de monitor heeft berekend en geen herafleiding. Een geseede Monte-Carlosimulatie van de statistiek zelf zit als derde controle in de tests; ze zit bewust NIET in het rapport, omdat een bemonsterde schatting trager zou zijn en niet bit voor bit reproduceerbaar. ExternalDataset wordt afgewezen: de grootheid is het detectievermogen van DEZE monitor bij de sigma-toewijzing van DIT scenario, en de sigmagroottes waarmee de MDB wordt geschaald zijn representatieve invoer uit een model en geen metingen. ReferenceImpl is ook overwogen en afgewezen — de Monte-Carlosimulatie bemonstert dezelfde statistiek als de analyse beschrijft, dus ze vangt transcriptie- en coëfficiëntfouten maar is geen onafhankelijke implementatie van de monitor. { "0.30" } ronde { "2" } (preregistratie 0135a9e4), geblokkeerd (blijft MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduceren de drempel van het rapport (binnen { "5.4e-12" }), de MDB per as, de vermogenscurve, het kruisingsveelvoud en de tijd tot detectie van de ramp (binnen { "5.3e-10" }) op vijf configuraties; de statistieken van geïnjecteerde fouten hebben geen onafhankelijke referentie en de sigmagroottes zijn invoer uit een model, dus de afbakening wacht op een besluit van de eigenaar

# The engine's own sdr correlator (sdr::correlate / synth_if / CaCode) stepped forward on seeded synthetic IF at a calibrated C/N₀ — a separate code path reaching the same numbers by numerical correlation of sampled IQ rather than by an algebraic jitter expression, so it is a different route and not a restatement — plus standard tabulated modified-Bessel I₀ values for the cycle-slip term. Loop theory per Kaplan & Hegarty ch. { "8" } and Viterbi/Gardner for the slip time. The row stays ReferenceImpl/MODELLED: the cross-check lives in this same codebase, and the bessel_i0 check validates one special function rather than the tracking model, so promoting the row on that basis would be self-serving labelling. ExternalDataset would need a recorded raw-IF dataset with ground-truth C/N₀ and an annotated loss-of-lock instant (TEXBAT/OAKBAT class); none ships in this tree, and even with the IQ those datasets publish no per-epoch loop-state truth, so a declared loss-of-lock time could only be validated against some other receiver's lock detector, which is a modelled choice and not an oracle. The loop bandwidths, integration time, correlator spacing, pull-in ratio, dwells, jammer power and antenna gains are representative band figures, not a datasheet. { "0.30" } round { "2" }, GNSS-SDR { "0.0.19" } (GPL-3.0, run as a program) on independently generated IF (pre-registered 6ef7698c, amendments to the measurement method disclosed), a finding (stays MODELLED): closed-loop PLL and DLL jitter agree within { "8" } % at { "35" } to { "45" } dB-Hz, the { "10" } Hz/s dynamic stress within { "1" } % and the first-order code ramp lag within { "2" } %; at { "30" } dB-Hz the PLL jitter is { "21" } % higher and the DLL jitter { "32" } % lower, the measured 15-degree-rule thresholds sit { "1.5" } dB above the engine, and the second-order loop slips { "0.5" } to { "2" } decades more often than the first-order slip-time bound. { "0.30" } revision: the Costas slip time now uses the loop SNR rho/4 (half-cycle slips); the default jitter_table log10_mean_time_to_cycle_slip_s falls from { "11.58" } to { "2.10" } at the { "25.47" } dB-Hz threshold

# An independent in-repo route. Every Jacobian row is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s evaluated from the state's own Earth-fixed and Moon-body-fixed coordinates rotated forward through the frame chain — a path that computes no derivative and shares no expression with the analytic partials — and the information matrices the two routes build agree to < { "1e-6" } of the largest diagonal. The linear-algebra kernel this composes (information_matrix, crlb, sym_eig) is separately externally anchored against numpy.linalg.eigh / numpy.linalg.inv in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs, and the delay observable carries lunar_vlbi's own delta-DOR far-field oracle; the row does not borrow either status. Closed-form identities checked here and labelled as such: σ² and 1/N scaling, and √(p/trace(M)) as an AM-HM lower bound the computed sigma cannot beat, which coincides with the equipartition value only on an isotropic geometry — which is why the measured ratio is exactly the anisotropy the assumption discarded. { "0.30" } external comparison (Library: NAIF SPICE CSPICE N0067 via spiceypy { "8.2.0" }, MIT; DE440, ITRF93 earth_latest_high_prec, MOON_PA), pre-registered ({ "50863551" }) together with an engine-side model-error sensitivity run: every Jacobian row is rebuilt by central differences of converged Newtonian light-time differences from SPICE geometry (the light-time equation is a short script over SPICE positions; SPICE carries the geometry), with the schedule rebuilt from SPICE elevations. The schedule, rank, defect, information spectrum, condition number, station sigmas, headline, trace bound, equipartition ratios, free-network null space, line-of-sight sweep and beacon declination agree within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.1" } deg, which sits above the engine known analytic-Moon error ({ "3.2e-3" } on sigmas, measured before the run). NumPy { "2.3.5" } redoes the linear algebra on the committed engine Jacobian to { "2e-13" } (P2). Validated as a Cramér-Rao bound for the stated reduced parameter set on the stated illustrative inputs: the Moon-centre ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held fixed in both legs, so it is not a real-campaign accuracy

# Closed-form identities and internal consistency; NO external oracle is claimed. The limb half angle is checked against asin(R_earth/r_orbit) and the geometry against it; the free-space loss, the DOP kernel and the SGP4 propagation this composes are each externally validated in their own rows and this row does not borrow their status. WHY MODELLED, and it is the transmit pattern: the gain at angle is a uniformly illuminated circular aperture, the Airy pattern, while a real GPS L1 antenna is a twelve-element helical array with a shaped main lobe peaked off-boresight to even out power across the Earth disc and sidelobes that are not Airy. Published measured patterns exist and are not vendored here, so orderings and orders of magnitude carry, while the dB of any single satellite does not. A FIRST IMPLEMENTATION OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING: the Airy expression is valid only in the forward hemisphere, its argument goes as sin(theta), and at theta near { "180" } deg it wraps around and returns FULL BORESIGHT GAIN behind the aperture - so the two strongest links in the first report were satellites pointing their antennas away from the Moon, and they were the only two that cleared the threshold. Behind the aperture the model now applies a flat back-lobe floor, stated as a bound rather than a fabricated pattern shape. Deliberately absent, each making the budget OPTIMISTIC: no ionospheric or tropospheric loss on the limb-grazing rays, no polarisation, pointing or implementation loss, and a spherical Earth with no refractive extension. The Moon position is an INPUT, not an ephemeris lookup, because the quantity under test is the link and the beam geometry. Upgrading this row to Validated needs a measured transmit pattern and LuGRE normal points to check against; neither is in the repository and neither is invented here. { "0.30" } round { "2" }, a finding (stays MODELLED): a measured-input link path (real SP3 positions, per-block transmit power, tabulated transmit and receive patterns, acquisition and tracking hysteresis) was checked against Montenbruck et al. { "2023" } Table { "3" } for GENESIS at { "6000" } km (pre-registered 5b414f0a): zenith-antenna means agree on { "5" } of { "6" } within one dB of the paper sensitivity; nadir-antenna means are { "1.0" } to { "2.4" } satellites higher than printed, plausibly because the paper used azimuth-dependent GPS side-lobe patterns that are not published. The lunar-distance figures of this row still use the Airy stand-in. { "0.30" }+ D7, a finding (stays MODELLED): the restated quantity, the C/N0 DIFFERENCE between Block IIR/IIR-M satellites tracked at one epoch, predicted with the NAVCEN per-SVN measured L1 patterns (two-dimensional, yaw-steering frame) against the LuGRE flight C/N0 (pre-registered 768cb62b: { "99" } % visible, pair RMS <= { "3.0" } dB, median <= { "2.0" } dB, at least { "50" } records and { "30" } pairs) has too little data: the { "26" } qualifying transit and lunar-orbit epochs hold { "15" } tracked IIR/IIR-M records (mostly IIF and III are tracked) and one pair. All { "15" } are predicted visible and the single pair's residual is -0.06 dB; nothing is claimed

Binnen deze rij wordt de verbetering zelf gecontroleerd tegen een onafhankelijk DOP-pad in de repository en vastgezet aan een vastgelegde golden. WAAROM MODELLED: het constellatieontwerp, de plaatsing van de bakens, de antennehoogten en alle drie de groottes van het foutenbudget zijn illustratieve invoer en geen in gebruik zijnde opmeting of gemeten link, dus de gerapporteerde meters zijn een eigenschap van een gekozen scenario en niet van een ingezette dienst. OPMERKING OVER HERKOMST: deze mogelijkheid werd in de README aangekondigd en door twee validatietests beproefd, maar had GEEN matrixrij en geen scenariosoort, dus ze was vanuit een run helemaal niet te bereiken. Ze werd in proza beweerd, ontbrak in het register en was in de engine onbereikbaar, alles tegelijk. De rij en de soort zijn samen toegevoegd. Ronde { "2" } van { "0.30" } (vooraf vastgelegd db0eb9ec), geblokkeerd (blijft MODELLED): zichtbaarheid met ANISE { "0.10.6" } en een numpy-DOP op de rijgeometrie komen overeen (zichtbare verzamelingen exact, inclusief { "72" } gevallen vlak bij de horizon en { "48" } vlak bij het masker; DOP binnen { "7.2e-13" }), maar de gerapporteerde meters zijn DOP maal een illustratieve wortel uit de kwadratensom van de afstandsfout van { "1.158" } m zonder referentie; de afbakening die ze zou uitsluiten wacht op een besluit van de eigenaar

# Closed-form identities and an independent in-repo route, with no external oracle claimed. The Helmert design — the only new derivative in the module — is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_frame_realise::apply_helmert, a path sharing no expression with the analytic form. The accumulation it composes is lunar-vlbi-fim's, whose Jacobian is finite-differenced against lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s there, and whose linear-algebra kernel is separately externally anchored against numpy in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs — this row borrows neither status. The physics oracle is structural rather than numerical: a beacon delay partial is the near-field DIFFERENCE of two near-parallel unit vectors, so the worst-determined translation direction MUST be the body-fixed direction to Earth, and the test asserts the computed answer against the separately measured direction. MEASURED: campaign-derived translation sigma { "6.3975" } m against the injected-transform scenario's { "0.3125" } m recovery error ({ "20.5" }× tighter), while rotation and scale run the OTHER way at { "2.32" }× and { "13.5" }× looser — the injected path assumes one isotropic sigma and so spreads its error uniformly across seven parameters, which the delay observable does not. Rank 7/7 but condition { "3.8e5" }, with one direction { "99.57" } % pure translation-toward-Earth and forty times worse than any other. WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT CLAIM: that the campaign figure is right in absolute terms. It establishes that the figure now DERIVES from a schedule, a geometry and an error model rather than from a planted answer. Stays ReferenceImpl/MODELLED: no published lunar-VLBI campaign-plus-datum-covariance pair exists, the station network and delay sigma are ILLUSTRATIVE inputs (the beacon catalogue is sourced, the campaign is not), observations are treated as independent while a real session's troposphere and clock are correlated between nearby scans, and the ephemeris, clocks, troposphere and Earth-orientation parameters are held FIXED. { "0.30" } external comparison, a finding (stays MODELLED): with every beacon Jacobian row rebuilt from NAIF SPICE light times (DE440, ITRF93, MOON_ME; pre-registered { "50863551" }), the schedule, Helmert rank, datum sigmas (within { "4.7e-3" }), condition, weakest direction ({ "0.07" } deg), libration sweep and arc ordering agree on six scenarios within the pre-registered { "1" } % / { "0.2" } / { "0.05" } deg. # Internal consistency: the brute-force elevation scan, the hand-computed and orbit::dop reductions, and the closed-form Walker and geosynchronous geometry. Two-body orbits with an optional secular J2, a spherical body with the local vertical along the radius, geometric visibility only (no signal power, satellite health, terrain or third-body perturbation), and the relative phase between systems taken from different reference epochs, so it is not a snapshot of any date. { "0.30" } review, a finding (stays MODELLED): the presets meet the published one-sided DOP-availability floors (BeiDou OS Performance Standard { "3.0" }, Galileo OS SDD) at { "100" } %, but with the engine's PDOP doubled in a scratch copy they still meet them (worst point { "99.36" } %), so the floors cannot detect even a { "100" } % error in the quantity this row computes; tightening them after the run would be a post-hoc tolerance. Promotion needs a two-sided comparison of DOP values on identical geometry with a pre-set tolerance. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (tests/coverage_dop_full_oracle.rs, pre-registered add671d6), a finding (stays MODELLED): Orekit { "12.2" } positions, elevation, azimuth and ground tracks with gnss_lib_py { "1.0.4" } GDOP/PDOP/HDOP/VDOP (common clock) and a numpy inverse (one clock per constellation) agree on every bar on the Galileo, GPS, BeiDou, Galileo+GPS (one clock each) and a 1584-satellite Earth design (DOP within { "2e-14" } relative, identical fix, availability and visibility counts). On the Moon and Mars designs the elements, positions, visibility and tracks agree, but DOP exceeds the pre-set { "1e-9" } relative bar in cells whose largest PDOP is { "1434" } or more (up to { "8e-6" }), and { "4" } cells differ by one fix epoch on numerically singular geometry (engine pivot { "1e-10" } against numpy rank), so the row is not validated. { "0.30" } revisions: the TDOP/GDOP reference clock is now the lowest-numbered constellation in view (constellation-multi-gnss-coverage global GDOP max { "2.2236" } -> { "2.1205" }, mean { "1.122" } -> { "1.131" }), and per-constellation visible counts are summed as integers
seg-2c0f28426ab9 = Interne consistentie: de brute-force elevatiescan, de met de hand berekende reducties en die van orbit::dop, en de gesloten vormen van Walker- en geosynchrone geometrie. Tweelichamenbanen met een optionele seculiere J2, een bolvormig lichaam met de lokale verticaal langs de straal, alleen geometrische zichtbaarheid (geen signaalvermogen, satellietgezondheid, terrein of storing door derde lichamen), en de relatieve fase tussen systemen overgenomen van verschillende referentietijdstippen, dus het is geen momentopname van enige datum. Review van { "0.30" }, een bevinding (blijft MODELLED): de voorinstellingen halen de gepubliceerde eenzijdige ondergrenzen voor DOP-beschikbaarheid (BeiDou OS Performance Standard { "3.0" }, Galileo OS SDD) bij { "100" } %, maar met de PDOP van de engine verdubbeld in een werkkopie halen ze die nog steeds (slechtste punt { "99.36" } %), zodat de ondergrenzen zelfs een fout van { "100" } % in de grootheid die deze rij berekent niet kunnen detecteren; ze na de run aanscherpen zou een post-hoc tolerantie zijn. Promotie vereist een tweezijdige vergelijking van DOP-waarden op identieke geometrie met een vooraf vastgestelde tolerantie. Ronde { "2" } van { "0.30" } (tests/coverage_dop_full_oracle.rs, vooraf vastgelegd add671d6), een bevinding (blijft MODELLED): Orekit { "12.2" } posities, elevatie, azimut en grondsporen met gnss_lib_py { "1.0.4" } GDOP/PDOP/HDOP/VDOP (gemeenschappelijke klok) en een numpy-inverse (één klok per constellatie) komen op elke drempel overeen voor de ontwerpen Galileo, GPS, BeiDou, Galileo+GPS (elk één klok) en een aardontwerp van 1584 satellieten (DOP binnen { "2e-14" } relatief, identieke positiebepaling, beschikbaarheid en zichtbaarheidsaantallen). Bij de ontwerpen voor de maan en Mars komen de elementen, posities, zichtbaarheid en sporen overeen, maar DOP overschrijdt de vooraf vastgestelde relatieve drempel van { "1e-9" } in cellen waarvan de grootste PDOP { "1434" } of meer is (tot { "8e-6" }), en { "4" } cellen verschillen één positiebepalingstijdstip op numeriek singuliere geometrie (pivot van de engine { "1e-10" } tegen de rang van numpy), dus de rij is niet getoetst. Revisies van { "0.30" }: de referentieklok voor TDOP/GDOP is nu de laagstgenummerde constellatie in zicht (constellation-multi-gnss-coverage globale GDOP-max { "2.2236" } -> { "2.1205" }, gemiddelde { "1.122" } -> { "1.131" }), en de zichtbare aantallen per constellatie worden als gehele getallen opgeteld

# Measured: TU Graz IfG/ITSG operational reduced-dynamic orbits (free for any use with acknowledgment; SHA-256 values in the fixture NOTICE) of GRACE-A, GRACE-C, Sentinel-2A and Sentinel-6A are the truth, fitted over { "20" } and { "30" } min arcs, and compared with the 22-parameter rows (a_dot, n_dot, Crs3, Crc3, Crs1, Crc1) of Tables { "4" }, { "5" }, { "6" } and { "8" } of Liu, Su, Xie, Zhou and Qu ({ "2025" }), Remote Sensing { "17" }({ "16" }):{ "2894" }, doi 10.3390/rs17162894 (CC BY { "4.0" }; numbers cited, not vendored). The round-2 re-run was pre-registered (aa559785) before the engine change and before the held-out orbits were fetched: the paper Eq. { "1" } and Section { "2.3" } fit, plus two new held-out days per satellite against the same printed values. Measured: every paper-day ratio { "0.755" } to { "1.138" }, and every held-out ratio { "0.683" } to { "1.186" }. Disclosed: the first run { "1.71" } (Sentinel-2A) and { "1.86" } (Sentinel-6A) { "20" } min along-track ratios were known before the re-run. The lowest held-out SISRE ratios sit near the { "0.667" } lower bound because the paper printed SISRE uses Table { "2" } weights unsquared (see the 16-parameter fitter row). The pre-registration mis-stated the paper stop rule (the paper stops on an RMS change below { "0.1" } mm); the pre-registered parameter-convergence rule was kept. Mutation: restoring zero-centred priors gives Sentinel-6A { "20" } min along-track { "1.08" } against { "0.70" } cm ({ "1.54" }), which fails. Reviewer probe: the pre-0.30 convention (a factor 1/2 on n_dot) leaves every figure unchanged, so the comparison constrains the function space of the 22-parameter model and the quality of its fit, not the n_dot convention. Outside the claim: the ATOMIC ECEF polynomial and the synthetic five-altitude model-comparison table, which have their own MODELLED row

# WHAT IS EXTERNALLY CHECKED, stated narrowly: against two independent open-source parsers (oracles: pycef { "1.11" } (PyPI, MIT); the Logstash CEF codec (logstash-codec-cef { "6.2.7" } in Logstash { "8.15.3" })), on a corpus of CEF lines this crate wrote ({ "157" } before amendment { "4" } below, { "170" } after it, { "176" } after amendment { "5" }): every band change and every epoch of a committed 84-line synthetic trust stream, plus adversarial text in hosts, reasons, time labels and gate names containing equals signs, backslashes, backslash-equals, pipes, line feeds, carriage returns, tabs, runs of spaces, multi-byte and astral characters and key lookalikes, extreme scores and offsets, every band with and without a previous band. pycef parses the lines whose header needs no escaping ({ "153" } before amendment { "4" }, { "166" } after it, { "172" } after amendment { "5" }) and recovers, exactly, the header fields, the severity, the exact extension key set and every value (numbers by f64 equality), after the CEF specification's unescape rules, which pycef does not apply and which scripts/gen_cef_ref.py applies. The Logstash codec, which unescapes by itself, parses every line ({ "157" }, then { "170" }, then { "176" }), meets the same bar, and also recovers four header fields that need escaping (a pipe, a backslash and an equals sign in the version string), which pycef's header pattern cannot read, so header escaping rests on the Logstash codec alone. The comparison bar was registered in commits { "5172e579" } (pycef) and 0a7598aa (Logstash) before either parser was run, and both met it at the first comparison. Five amendments are disclosed. Amendments ({ "1" }) to ({ "3" }) touched no comparison rule and no expected value: ({ "1" }) a corpus-size assertion was corrected from { "5" } to { "4" } band changes, a miscount, since the stream has { "4" }; ({ "2" }) the Logstash runner's pipeline delimiter wiring was fixed after a first attempt that produced no events; ({ "3" }) the trust score and epoch offset were moved from the CEF integer fields cn1 and cn2 to the floating-point fields cfp1 and cfp2, a wire-format change made in this release, after which the corpus was regenerated and both oracles were re-run with the bar unchanged and met it again. Amendment ({ "4" }), { "0.36" }, extended the corpus and the expected key set, registered in d6331429 before either parser ran on the new lines: schema { "1.3" } inputs add the satellite geometry in the custom fields cs6 (label geometry: status, then ';' and the deduction classes), cfp3 (geometryHdop) and cfp4 (geometryPdop); { "13" } geometry cases (the corpus to { "170" } lines) and the three new keys; both met the bar, otherwise unchanged, at the first comparison (pycef { "166" } of { "170" } lines parsed, the { "4" } tier { "2" } header cases as before; Logstash { "170" } of { "170" }). Amendment ({ "5" }), { "0.36" }, appended { "6" } geometry-label cases (the corpus to { "176" } lines), registered in 9a8e4fa9 before either parser ran on them: from inputs that carry the geometry label, cs6 gains a third field (';' and the label) and cn1 (geometryUsedSatellites) the satellites used; the Logstash codec's mapping table was read first (no flexString fields, hence cs6 rather than flexString1); both met the bar at the first comparison (pycef { "172" } of { "176" }, Logstash { "176" } of { "176" }), and every earlier reference entry was reproduced unchanged. WHAT IS NOT: neither parser type-checks the cfp values (both keep them as text), so the use of the floating-point fields rests on the CEF dictionary rather than on an oracle; no vendor SIEM was run; the extension key names and their mapping are this crate's choice, and the parsers show only that they are read back as written; the RFC { "5424" } envelope, delivery, timeouts and reconnection are not covered; pycef is a small parser last released in { "2020" }, and the Logstash codec is the more substantial oracle.

Its Type I minimum C3 is not matched: { "10.535" } vs { "11.11" } (-5.18 %), where the library oracle agrees with Kshana. That miss is recorded and asserted.

# checked against its own closed-form / analytic identity — catches transcription and coefficient errors, but is not an external oracle — Closed-form identities and internal consistency; NO external oracle is claimed. The limb half angle is checked against asin(R_earth/r_orbit) and the geometry against it; the free-space loss, the DOP kernel and the SGP4 propagation this composes are each externally validated in their own rows and this row does not borrow their status. WHY MODELLED, and it is the transmit pattern: the gain at angle is a uniformly illuminated circular aperture, the Airy pattern, while a real GPS L1 antenna is a twelve-element helical array with a shaped main lobe peaked off-boresight to even out power across the Earth disc and sidelobes that are not Airy. Published measured patterns exist and are not vendored here, so orderings and orders of magnitude carry, while the dB of any single satellite does not. A FIRST IMPLEMENTATION OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING: the Airy expression is valid only in the forward hemisphere, its argument goes as sin(theta), and at theta near { "180" } deg it wraps around and returns FULL BORESIGHT GAIN behind the aperture - so the two strongest links in the first report were satellites pointing their antennas away from the Moon, and they were the only two that cleared the threshold. Behind the aperture the model now applies a flat back-lobe floor, stated as a bound rather than a fabricated pattern shape. Deliberately absent, each making the budget OPTIMISTIC: no ionospheric or tropospheric loss on the limb-grazing rays, no polarisation, pointing or implementation loss, and a spherical Earth with no refractive extension. The Moon position is an INPUT, not an ephemeris lookup, because the quantity under test is the link and the beam geometry. Upgrading this row to Validated needs a measured transmit pattern and LuGRE normal points to check against; neither is in the repository and neither is invented here. { "0.30" } round { "2" }, a finding (stays MODELLED): a measured-input link path (real SP3 positions, per-block transmit power, tabulated transmit and receive patterns, acquisition and tracking hysteresis) was checked against Montenbruck et al. { "2023" } Table { "3" } for GENESIS at { "6000" } km (pre-registered 5b414f0a): zenith-antenna means agree on { "5" } of { "6" } within one dB of the paper sensitivity; nadir-antenna means are { "1.0" } to { "2.4" } satellites higher than printed, plausibly because the paper used azimuth-dependent GPS side-lobe patterns that are not published. The lunar-distance figures of this row still use the Airy stand-in. { "0.30" }+ D7, a finding (stays MODELLED): the restated quantity, the C/N0 DIFFERENCE between Block IIR/IIR-M satellites tracked at one epoch, predicted with the NAVCEN per-SVN measured L1 patterns (two-dimensional, yaw-steering frame) against the LuGRE flight C/N0 (pre-registered 768cb62b: { "99" } % visible, pair RMS <= { "3.0" } dB, median <= { "2.0" } dB, at least { "50" } records and { "30" } pairs) has too little data: the { "26" } qualifying transit and lunar-orbit epochs hold { "15" } tracked IIR/IIR-M records (mostly IIF and III are tracked) and one pair. All { "15" } are predicted visible and the single pair's residual is -0.06 dB; nothing is claimed
seg-ed40da9bdfae = getoetst aan de eigen gesloten-vorm-/analytische identiteit — vangt transcriptie- en coëfficiëntfouten op, maar is geen externe onafhankelijke referentie — Gesloten-vormidentiteiten en interne consistentie; er wordt GEEN externe onafhankelijke referentie geclaimd. De limb-halve hoek wordt gecontroleerd tegen asin(R_earth/r_orbit) en de geometrie daartegen; het vrijeruimteverlies, de DOP-kernel en de SGP4-propagatie die dit samenstelt zijn elk extern getoetst in hun eigen rijen en deze rij leent hun status niet. WAAROM MODELLED, en het is het zendpatroon: de gain onder een hoek is een uniform verlichte cirkelvormige apertuur, het Airy-patroon, terwijl een echte GPS L1-antenne een helixarray van twaalf elementen is met een gevormde hoofdlob die buiten de boresight piekt om het vermogen over de aardschijf gelijk te trekken en met zijlobben die niet Airy zijn. Gepubliceerde gemeten patronen bestaan en zijn hier niet meegeleverd, dus rangordes en ordes van grootte gaan op, terwijl de dB van een enkele satelliet dat niet doet. EEN EERSTE IMPLEMENTATIE VAN DEZE RIJ WAS FOUT OP EEN MANIER DIE HET VASTLEGGEN WAARD IS: de Airy-uitdrukking is alleen geldig in de voorwaartse halfruimte, haar argument gaat als sin(theta), en bij theta nabij { "180" } graden slaat ze om en geeft VOLLEDIGE BORESIGHT-GAIN achter de apertuur - zodat de twee sterkste links in het eerste rapport satellieten waren die hun antennes van de Maan af richtten, en zij waren de enige twee die de drempel haalden. Achter de apertuur past het model nu een vlakke achterlobvloer toe, vermeld als grens in plaats van een verzonnen patroonvorm. Bewust afwezig, elk met als gevolg dat het budget OPTIMISTISCH is: geen ionosferisch of troposferisch verlies op de limb-rakende stralen, geen polarisatie-, richt- of implementatieverlies, en een bolvormige Aarde zonder refractieve uitbreiding. De Maanpositie is een INVOER, geen efemeridenopzoeking, omdat de grootheid die wordt getoetst de link en de bundelgeometrie is. Deze rij opwaarderen tot onafhankelijk getoetst vereist een gemeten zendpatroon en LuGRE-normaalpunten om tegen te toetsen; geen van beide staat in de repository en geen van beide wordt hier verzonnen. { "0.30" } ronde { "2" }, een bevinding (blijft MODELLED): een link-pad met gemeten invoer (echte SP3-posities, zendvermogen per blok, getabelleerde zend- en ontvangstpatronen, acquisitie- en trackinghysterese) is gecontroleerd tegen Montenbruck et al. { "2023" } Tabel { "3" } voor GENESIS op { "6000" } km (vooraf geregistreerd 5b414f0a): gemiddelden met zenitantenne komen bij { "5" } van { "6" } binnen één dB van de gevoeligheid uit het artikel overeen; gemiddelden met nadirantenne liggen { "1.0" } tot { "2.4" } satellieten hoger dan afgedrukt, vermoedelijk omdat het artikel azimutafhankelijke GPS-zijlobpatronen gebruikte die niet zijn gepubliceerd. De cijfers op maanafstand van deze rij gebruiken nog steeds de Airy-vervanger. { "0.30" }+ D7, een bevinding (blijft MODELLED): de herformuleerde grootheid, het C/N0-VERSCHIL tussen Block IIR/IIR-M-satellieten gevolgd op één epoch, voorspeld met de gemeten NAVCEN L1-patronen per SVN (tweedimensionaal, yaw-sturingsframe) tegen de LuGRE-vlucht-C/N0 (vooraf geregistreerd 768cb62b: { "99" } % zichtbaar, paar-RMS <= { "3.0" } dB, mediaan <= { "2.0" } dB, minstens { "50" } records en { "30" } paren) heeft te weinig data: de { "26" } kwalificerende transit- en maanbaanepochs bevatten { "15" } gevolgde IIR/IIR-M-records (meestal worden IIF en III gevolgd) en één paar. ({ "1" }) Closed-form round trip: the minimum detectable bias is produced by inverting the non-central chi-square tail on the non-centrality (raim::pbias) and is verified by feeding the resulting non-centrality back through raim::noncentral_chi2_cdf at the monitor's own threshold, which must return P_md. ({ "2" }) Injection vs analysis: a bias is written into the RF estimate of one axis and cross_raim::run_cross_raim is re-run, so the statistic compared against the analytic non-centrality is the one the monitor computed, not a re-derivation. A seeded Monte-Carlo of the statistic itself is carried in the tests as a third check; it is deliberately NOT in the report, because a sampled estimate would be slower and not reproducible bit-for-bit. ExternalDataset is declined: the quantity is the detection power of THIS monitor at THIS scenario's sigma allocation, and the sigma magnitudes the MDB is scaled by are Modelled representative inputs, not measurements. ReferenceImpl was also considered and declined — the Monte-Carlo samples the same statistic the analysis describes, so it catches transcription and coefficient errors but is not an independent implementation of the monitor. { "0.30" } round { "2" } (pre-registered 0135a9e4), blocked (stays MODELLED): SciPy { "1.18.1" } chi2/ncx2/brentq reproduce the report threshold (within { "5.4e-12" }), per-axis MDB, power curve, crossing multiple and ramp time-to-detect (within { "5.3e-10" }) on five configurations; the injected-fault statistics have no independent oracle and the sigma magnitudes are Modelled inputs, so the scoping awaits an owner decision

# checked against a separate implementation in this same codebase — independent of the unit under test, but not externally authoritative — Closed-form identities and an independent in-repo route, with no external oracle claimed. The Helmert design — the only new derivative in the module — is re-derived by central finite difference of lunar_frame_realise::apply_helmert, a path sharing no expression with the analytic form. The accumulation it composes is lunar-vlbi-fim's, whose Jacobian is finite-differenced against lunar_vlbi::vlbi_delay_s there, and whose linear-algebra kernel is separately externally anchored against numpy in tests/fim_observability_reference.rs — this row borrows neither status. The physics oracle is structural rather than numerical: a beacon delay partial is the near-field DIFFERENCE of two near-parallel unit vectors, so the worst-determined translation direction MUST be the body-fixed direction to Earth, and the test asserts the computed answer against the separately measured direction. # <b>What the observatory does instead.</b> The question the almanac comparison answers is "could the constellation geometry alone explain this low accuracy?". The base method answers a related question without an almanac: a low share must stand out against the same day's background across the region and against the aircraft's own good reports elsewhere. That is a different and weaker substitute: it cannot say that a quiet-geometry hour was degraded where the day's background is also low. An almanac-HDOP scorer is <b>not implemented</b>. It is an open item, to be built from the paper's full text and the public GPS almanac and pre-registered before it is run; until then it is cited, not claimed.
seg-4f8471649a07 = <b>Wat het observatorium in plaats daarvan doet.</b> De vraag die de almanakvergelijking beantwoordt is "kan de constellatiegeometrie alleen deze lage nauwkeurigheid verklaren?". De basismethode beantwoordt een verwante vraag zonder almanak: een laag aandeel moet opvallen tegenover de achtergrond van dezelfde dag in de regio en tegenover de eigen goede meldingen van het vliegtuig elders. Dat is een ander en zwakker substituut: het kan niet zeggen dat een uur met rustige geometrie verslechterd was waar ook de achtergrond van de dag laag is. Een almanak-HDOP-scorer is <b>niet geïmplementeerd</b>. Het is een openstaand punt, te bouwen uit de volledige tekst van het artikel en de openbare GPS-almanak en vooraf vast te leggen voordat het wordt uitgevoerd; tot die tijd wordt het aangehaald, niet geclaimd.
